This is the limit\n# for flow allocation inside the engine. You can change this value to allow\n# more memory usage for flows.\n# The hash-size determines the size of the hash used to identify flows inside\n# the engine, and by default the value is 65536.\n# At startup, the engine can preallocate a number of flows, to get better\n# performance. The number of flows preallocated is 10000 by default.\n# emergency-recovery is the percentage of flows that the engine needs to\n# prune before clearing the emergency state. The emergency state is activated\n# when the memcap limit is reached, allowing new flows to be created, but\n# pruning them with the emergency timeouts (they are defined below).\n# If the memcap is reached, the engine will try to prune flows\n# with the default timeouts. If it doesn't find a flow to prune, it will set\n# the emergency bit and it will try again with more aggressive timeouts.\n# If that doesn't work, then it will try to kill the oldest flows using\n# last time seen flows.\n# The memcap can be specified in kb, mb, gb. Here you can specify the timeouts that the\n# active flows will wait to transit from the current state to another, on each\n# protocol. The value of \"new\" determines the seconds to wait after a handshake or\n# stream startup before the engine frees the data of that flow it doesn't\n# change the state to established (usually if we don't receive more packets\n# of that flow). The value of \"established\" is the amount of\n# seconds that the engine will wait to free the flow if that time elapses\n# without receiving new packets or closing the connection. \"closed\" is the\n# amount of time to wait after a flow is closed (usually zero). \"bypassed\"\n# timeout controls locally bypassed flows. For these flows we don't do any other\n# tracking. If no packets have been seen after this timeout, the flow is discarded.\n#\n# There's an emergency mode that will become active under attack circumstances,\n# making the engine to check flow status faster. The engine\n# allows us to specify the profile to use for them, to manage memory in an\n# efficient way keeping good performance. For the profile keyword you\n# can use the words \"low\", \"medium\", \"high\" or \"custom\". If you use custom,\n# make sure to define the values in the \"custom-values\" section.\n# Usually you would prefer medium/high/low.\n#\n# \"sgh mpm-context\", indicates how the staging should allot mpm contexts for\n# the signature groups.  \"single\" indicates the use of a single context for\n# all the signature group heads.  \"full\" indicates a mpm-context for each\n# group head.  \"auto\" lets the engine decide the distribution of contexts\n# based on the information the engine gathers on the patterns from each\n# group head.\n#\n# The option inspection-recursion-limit is used to limit the recursive calls\n# in the content inspection code.  For certain payload-sig combinations, we\n# might end up taking too much time in the content inspection code.\n# If the argument specified is 0, there are no limits on the recursion.\n# When a value is not specified, the default is 3000\ndetect:\n  profile: medium\n  custom-values:\n    toclient-groups: 3\n    toserver-groups: 25\n  sgh-mpm-context: auto\n  #inspection-recursion-limit: 3000\n  # try to tie an app-layer transaction for rules without app-layer keywords\n  # if there is only one live transaction for the flow\n  # allows to log app-layer metadata in alert\n  # but the transaction may not be the relevant one.\n  # guess-applayer-tx: no\n  # If set to yes, the loading of signatures will be made after the capture\n  # is started. They consist of a rule header and rule options.\n\n## Basic Structure\n```\naction protocol source_ip source_port -> dest_ip dest_port (rule_options;)\n```\n\n### Action Keywords\n- `alert` - Generate an alert and log the packet\n- `drop` - Block the packet and alert (inline mode only)\n- `reject` - Close the connection\n- `pass` - Allow the traffic without alerting\n\n### Protocol Keywords\n- `http` - HTTP protocol (application layer)\n- `tcp` - TCP protocol\n- `udp` - UDP protocol\n- `ip` - All IP traffic\n\n### Direction Operators\n- `->` - Unidirectional (source to destination)\n- `<>` - Bidirectional\n\n### Network Variables\n- `$HOME_NET` - Internal networks\n- `$EXTERNAL_NET` - External networks\n- `any` - Any address\n- Specific IPs or CIDR ranges: `192.168.1.0/24`\n\n### Port Specifications\n- `any` - Any port\n- `80` - Specific port\n- `[80,443]` - Multiple ports\n- `!80` - Not port 80\n- `1024:` - Ports 1024 and above\n\n## Common Rule Options\n\n### Metadata and ID\n- `sid:XXXXXXX` - Signature ID (unique identifier)\n- `msg:\"description\"` - Human-readable alert message\n- `rev:1` - Rule revision number\n- `classtype:attempted-recon` - Classification\n- `priority:1` - Priority level (1-4)\n\n### Protocol-Specific Options\n\n#### HTTP Layer Options\n- `http.method` - HTTP method (POST, GET, etc.)\n- `http.uri` - Request URI/path\n- `http.host` - Host header\n- `http.request_line` - Full request line\n- `http.request_body` - Request body content\n- `http.header` - Any HTTP header\n- `http.user_agent` - User-Agent header\n\n#### Content Matching\n- `content:\"string\"` - Match exact string\n- `content:\"|48 54 54 50|\"` - Match hex bytes (HTTP in hex)\n- `nocase` - Case-insensitive matching\n- `http_client_body` - Restrict matching to HTTP request body\n- `depth:X` - Maximum distance from start\n- `offset:X` - Skip X bytes before matching\n- `distance:X` - Distance from previous content match\n- `within:X` - Maximum bytes from previous match\n\n### Logical Operators\n- `&&` - AND (all conditions must match)\n- `||` - OR (at least one condition must match)\n- `!` - NOT (negation)\n\n## Pattern Matching Examples\n\n### Base64 Detection\nBase64 characters are A-Z, a-z, 0-9, +, /, and = for padding.\nPattern for Base64-like content:\n```\ncontent:\"[A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,}\"\npcre:\"[A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,}\"\n```\n\n### Hex String Pattern\nFor exactly 64 hex characters (SHA256):\n```\npcre:\"[0-9A-Fa-f]{64}\"\n```\n\n### Named Parameters\nFor `blob=VALUE` patterns:\n```\ncontent:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/i\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n## Rule Writing Best Practices\n\n1. **Specificity First**: Start with the most specific criteria (method, URI, headers)\n2. **Narrow Content Matches**: Use anchors and patterns to avoid false positives\n3. **Use Modifiers**: Apply `http_client_body`, `nocase`, and other modifiers to improve accuracy\n4. **Test Patterns**: Validate regex patterns before deploying\n5. **Use Flow**: Use `flow:to_server` to ensure direction\n6. **Header Matching**: Use HTTP-specific options for headers, not generic content\n\n## Example Rule Structure\n\n```\nalert http $HOME_NET any -> $EXTERNAL_NET any (\n  msg:\"Suspicious HTTP POST\";\n  flow:to_server,established;\n  http.method:POST;\n  http.uri:\"/api/endpoint\";\n  http.header:\"X-Custom-Header|\"; content:\"value\";\n  http_client_body;\n  content:\"sensitive_keyword\";\n  sid:1000001;\n  rev:1;\n  priority:1;\n)\n```\n\n## Testing and Debugging\n\n### Validate Rule Syntax\n```bash\nsuricata -c /path/to/suricata.yaml -S /path/to/rules.rules\n```\n\n### Test Against PCAP\n```bash\nsuricata -c /path/to/suricata.yaml -r /path/to/test.pcap -S /path/to/rules.rules\n```\n\n### Check Alert Output\nLook in the output directory (usually `/var/log/suricata/eve.json`) for alerts.\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":6,"cache_creation_input_tokens":154,"cache_read_input_tokens":20419,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":154,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"58e82843-836a-4951-a231-f8576a5ff90b","uuid":"d0b20957-7b75-4f5b-bc70-3835e1ecab4c"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_0157UyWYhzLNaiguH2xyJ7Ho","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01AbVDmQghoVMUAsZj79YzrU","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/http-request-body-detection.md","content":"# HTTP Request Body Detection in Suricata\n\n## Overview\nDetecting patterns in HTTP POST request bodies requires understanding how Suricata parses and matches HTTP protocol elements.\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n### HTTP Request Body Matching\n- `http_client_body` - Restrict content matching to the HTTP request body only\n- `http.request_body` - Named buffer for request body content\n- Automatic decompression of gzip, deflate, etc. when configured\n\n### Buffer and Stream Handling\n- Suricata can examine the request body after parsing HTTP headers\n- The body starts after the double CRLF (`\\r\\n\\r\\n`)\n- Large bodies may be truncated based on config settings\n\n## Common Patterns for Form Data\n\n### URL-Encoded Form Data\nFormat: `key1=value1&key2=value2`\n```\ncontent:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n### Multipart Form Data\nDetected by `Content-Type: multipart/form-data` boundary\n\n### JSON Payloads\n```\ncontent:\"{\\\"blob\\\":\\\"\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n### Raw Binary Data\nCan contain any bytes, detected via hex patterns\n\n## Matching Parameters with Values\n\n### Pattern: `param=value`\nUse `content` with fixed prefix, then `pcre` for value pattern:\n\n```\ncontent:\"sig=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n### Multiple Parameter Matching\nCombine multiple content/pcre pairs for different parameters:\n\n```\ncontent:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+\\/]{80,})/\"; http_client_body;\ncontent:\"sig=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n### Parameter Order Independence\nBy default, content matches don't require order. To enforce sequence:\n```\ncontent:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\ncontent:\"sig=\"; http_client_body; distance:0; within:10000;\n```\n\n## Base64 Pattern Detection\n\n### Base64 Character Set\nValid Base64: `A-Z a-z 0-9 + / =`\n\n### Length Requirements\n- Minimum padding: Base64 output is always multiple of 4 chars\n- 80+ chars = at least 60 bytes of data (80 * 3/4)\n\n### Pattern Examples\n```\n# Match 80+ Base64 chars (with optional line breaks)\npcre:\"/[A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,}/\"; http_client_body;\n\n# Match allowing padding at end\npcre:\"/[A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,}={0,2}/\"; http_client_body;\n\n# Case variations\npcre:\"/[A-Za-z0-9+\\/]+/i\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n## Hex String Pattern Detection\n\n### SHA256/Hash Format\n- Exactly 64 hexadecimal characters\n- Case-insensitive: `[0-9a-fA-F]{64}` or `[0-9A-Fa-f]{64}`\n\n### Pattern for `sig=VALUE` with 64 hex chars\n```\ncontent:\"sig=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n## Regex Metacharacters in PCRE\n\n### Escaping Special Characters\n- `/` requires `\\/` when using `/pattern/` syntax\n- `+` means \"one or more\" (no escape needed in character class)\n- Character class `[]` groups characters for OR logic\n- `-` inside `[]` denotes range (use `\\-` or put at end to escape)\n\n### PCRE Syntax in Suricata\n```\npcre:\"/pattern/flags\";\n```\n\nValid flags:\n- `i` - Case insensitive\n- `s` - Single line (. matches newline)\n- `m` - Multiline (^ and $ match line boundaries)\n- `x` - Extended (ignore whitespace, allow comments)\n\n## Advanced: Content + PCRE Chains\n\n### Optimization Pattern\nUse `content` to anchor, then `pcre` for complex patterns:\n\n```\ncontent:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/i\"; http_client_body; distance:0; within:1000;\n```\n\nThe `content` match triggers the \"fast path\", then `pcre` validates the pattern.\n\n### Multiple Constraints\n```\ncontent:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/blob=(?:[A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/i\"; http_client_body;\ncontent:\"sig=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/sig=(?:[0-9a-fA-F]{64})/i\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n### Pitfall: Forgetting to Anchor Body Matches\nWithout `http_client_body`, content may match headers or other parts.\n\n### Pitfall: PCRE Escaping\nForward slashes in patterns must be escaped: `\\/` not `/`\n\n### Pitfall: Character Class Ranges\n```\nWRONG: /[a-z A-Z 0-9]/    # spaces are literal\nRIGHT: /[a-zA-Z0-9]/      # correct range syntax\n```\n\n### Pitfall: Greedy vs Non-Greedy\n- `{80,}` is greedy (matches as much as possible)\n- `{80,}?` is non-greedy (matches as little as possible)\n- Use non-greedy to stop at value boundaries\n\n## Testing Patterns\n\n### Validate PCRE Pattern\nUse online regex testers with your exact pattern:\n- Test string: sample Base64 and hex values\n- Verify character classes match intended characters\n- Check escaping is correct\n\n### Test in Suricata\n```bash\n# Check rule syntax\nsuricata -c /path/to/config.yaml -S /path/to/rules.rules -T\n\n# Test against PCAP\nsuricata -c /path/to/config.yaml -r /path/to/test.pcap -l /tmp/suricata\n```\n\n## Example Complete Rule\n\n```\nalert http $HOME_NET any -> $EXTERNAL_NET any (\n  msg:\"HTTP POST Exfil Pattern Detection\";\n  flow:to_server,established;\n  http.method:POST;\n  http.uri:\"/telemetry/v2/report\";\n  http.header:X-TLM-Mode|; content:\"exfil\";\n  http_client_body;\n  content:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\n  pcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/\"; http_client_body;\n  content:\"sig=\"; http_client_body;\n  pcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\"; http_client_body;\n  sid:1000001;\n  rev:1;\n  priority:1;\n  classtype:attempted-recon;\n)\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1459,"cache_read_input_tokens":20573,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1459,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"58e82843-836a-4951-a231-f8576a5ff90b","uuid":"258a84b7-4ddd-48de-87c0-506296b7a21d"}

http_client_body;\npcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n### Multipart Form Data\nDetected by `Content-Type: multipart/form-data` boundary\n\n### JSON Payloads\n```\ncontent:\"{\\\"blob\\\":\\\"\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n### Raw Binary Data\nCan contain any bytes, detected via hex patterns\n\n## Matching Parameters with Values\n\n### Pattern: `param=value`\nUse `content` with fixed prefix, then `pcre` for value pattern:\n\n```\ncontent:\"sig=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n### Multiple Parameter Matching\nCombine multiple content/pcre pairs for different parameters:\n\n```\ncontent:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+\\/]{80,})/\"; http_client_body;\ncontent:\"sig=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n### Parameter Order Independence\nBy default, content matches don't require order. To enforce sequence:\n```\ncontent:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\ncontent:\"sig=\"; http_client_body; distance:0; within:10000;\n```\n\n## Base64 Pattern Detection\n\n### Base64 Character Set\nValid Base64: `A-Z a-z 0-9 + / =`\n\n### Length Requirements\n- Minimum padding: Base64 output is always multiple of 4 chars\n- 80+ chars = at least 60 bytes of data (80 * 3/4)\n\n### Pattern Examples\n```\n# Match 80+ Base64 chars (with optional line breaks)\npcre:\"/[A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,}/\"; http_client_body;\n\n# Match allowing padding at end\npcre:\"/[A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,}={0,2}/\"; http_client_body;\n\n# Case variations\npcre:\"/[A-Za-z0-9+\\/]+/i\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n## Hex String Pattern Detection\n\n### SHA256/Hash Format\n- Exactly 64 hexadecimal characters\n- Case-insensitive: `[0-9a-fA-F]{64}` or `[0-9A-Fa-f]{64}`\n\n### Pattern for `sig=VALUE` with 64 hex chars\n```\ncontent:\"sig=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n## Regex Metacharacters in PCRE\n\n### Escaping Special Characters\n- `/` requires `\\/` when using `/pattern/` syntax\n- `+` means \"one or more\" (no escape needed in character class)\n- Character class `[]` groups characters for OR logic\n- `-` inside `[]` denotes range (use `\\-` or put at end to escape)\n\n### PCRE Syntax in Suricata\n```\npcre:\"/pattern/flags\";\n```\n\nValid flags:\n- `i` - Case insensitive\n- `s` - Single line (. matches newline)\n- `m` - Multiline (^ and $ match line boundaries)\n- `x` - Extended (ignore whitespace, allow comments)\n\n## Advanced: Content + PCRE Chains\n\n### Optimization Pattern\nUse `content` to anchor, then `pcre` for complex patterns:\n\n```\ncontent:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/i\"; http_client_body; distance:0; within:1000;\n```\n\nThe `content` match triggers the \"fast path\", then `pcre` validates the pattern.\n\n### Multiple Constraints\n```\ncontent:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/blob=(?:[A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/i\"; http_client_body;\ncontent:\"sig=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/sig=(?:[0-9a-fA-F]{64})/i\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n### Pitfall: Forgetting to Anchor Body Matches\nWithout `http_client_body`, content may match headers or other parts.\n\n### Pitfall: PCRE Escaping\nForward slashes in patterns must be escaped: `\\/` not `/`\n\n### Pitfall: Character Class Ranges\n```\nWRONG: /[a-z A-Z 0-9]/    # spaces are literal\nRIGHT: /[a-zA-Z0-9]/      # correct range syntax\n```\n\n### Pitfall: Greedy vs Non-Greedy\n- `{80,}` is greedy (matches as much as possible)\n- `{80,}?` is non-greedy (matches as little as possible)\n- Use non-greedy to stop at value boundaries\n\n## Testing Patterns\n\n### Validate PCRE Pattern\nUse online regex testers with your exact pattern:\n- Test string: sample Base64 and hex values\n- Verify character classes match intended characters\n- Check escaping is correct\n\n### Test in Suricata\n```bash\n# Check rule syntax\nsuricata -c /path/to/config.yaml -S /path/to/rules.rules -T\n\n# Test against PCAP\nsuricata -c /path/to/config.yaml -r /path/to/test.pcap -l /tmp/suricata\n```\n\n## Example Complete Rule\n\n```\nalert http $HOME_NET any -> $EXTERNAL_NET any (\n  msg:\"HTTP POST Exfil Pattern Detection\";\n  flow:to_server,established;\n  http.method:POST;\n  http.uri:\"/telemetry/v2/report\";\n  http.header:X-TLM-Mode|; content:\"exfil\";\n  http_client_body;\n  content:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\n  pcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/\"; http_client_body;\n  content:\"sig=\"; http_client_body;\n  pcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\"; http_client_body;\n  sid:1000001;\n  rev:1;\n  priority:1;\n  classtype:attempted-recon;\n)\n```\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01XevrevXB7UCqAXqvWbmKpZ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_017LUUfG8arrLfBCh3oUXfhY","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/http-header-matching.md","content":"# HTTP Header Matching in Suricata\n\n## Overview\nMatching specific HTTP headers is critical for distinguishing exfiltration traffic. Suricata provides multiple ways to match headers with different levels of specificity and performance.\n\n## Header Matching Methods\n\n### Method 1: http.header (Named Buffer)\nMatches any header by name:\n```\nhttp.header:X-Custom-Header|; content:\"value\";\n```\n\nThe pipe `|` terminates the header name and ensures we're matching the header value.\n\n### Method 2: Content with Header Anchor\n```\ncontent:\"X-Custom-Header|\"; http_header;\ncontent:\"value\"; http_header; distance:0; within:200;\n```\n\n### Method 3: PCRE for Header Values\n```\npcre:\"/X-Custom-Header:\\s+(.+?)$/m\"; http_header;\n```\n\n## Common HTTP Headers\n\n### Request Headers\n- `Host` - Server hostname\n- `User-Agent` - Client application identifier\n- `Content-Type` - Body format (application/json, application/x-www-form-urlencoded, etc.)\n- `Content-Length` - Size of request body\n- `Authorization` - Authentication credentials\n- `Accept` - Preferred response formats\n- `Accept-Encoding` - Accepted compression\n- `X-*` - Custom application headers\n\n### Header Value Characteristics\n- Header names are case-insensitive in HTTP\n- Header values are case-sensitive\n- Whitespace after colon varies by implementation\n\n## Matching Custom Headers\n\n### Pattern: `X-TLM-Mode: exfil`\n```\nhttp.header:X-TLM-Mode|; content:\"exfil\";\n```\n\nOr with PCRE:\n```\npcre:\"/X-TLM-Mode:\\s*exfil\\s*$/i\"; http_header;\n```\n\n### Header Presence Check\nTo check if a header exists (regardless of value):\n```\nhttp.header:X-TLM-Mode|;\n```\n\n### Case-Insensitive Header Values\n```\nhttp.header:X-TLM-Mode|; content:\"exfil\"; nocase;\n```\n\nOr with PCRE:\n```\npcre:\"/X-TLM-Mode:\\s*exfil/i\"; http_header;\n```\n\n## Matching Multiple Headers\n\n### All Headers Must Match (AND)\nUse content rules in sequence:\n```\nhttp.header:Host|; content:\"api.example.com\";\nhttp.header:X-Custom|; content:\"secret\";\n```\n\n### Multiple Options in Same Header\n```\nhttp.header:X-Mode|; content:\"exfil|debug\";\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n### Use Named Buffers for Clarity\n```\nPREFERRED: http.header:X-TLM-Mode|; content:\"exfil\";\nLESS CLEAR: content:\"X-TLM-Mode|exfil\"; http_header;\n```\n\n### Anchor Header Names with Pipe\nThe `|` character marks the end of the header name:\n```\nCORRECT: http.header:X-TLM-Mode|; content:\"exfil\";\nWRONG: http.header:X-TLM-Mode; content:\"exfil\";\n```\n\n### Combine with Method and URI\nHeader matching is more efficient when paired with specific method and path:\n```\nhttp.method:POST;\nhttp.uri:\"/telemetry/v2/report\";\nhttp.header:X-TLM-Mode|; content:\"exfil\";\n```\n\n## Escaping and Special Characters\n\n### Pipe Character in Header Names\nMost header names don't contain pipes, but if needed:\n```\nhttp.header:X-Some-Header|; content:\"value\";\n```\n\n### Special Values\n- Empty value: `content:\"\";`\n- Whitespace: `content:\" \";`\n- Exact length: Use `depth` and `offset`\n\n## Header Parsing Edge Cases\n\n### Whitespace Handling\nHTTP allows optional whitespace around values:\n```\nX-Header: value\nX-Header:  value  (extra spaces)\nX-Header:value (no space)\n```\n\nTo match flexibly:\n```\npcre:\"/X-TLM-Mode:\\s*exfil\\s*$/i\"; http_header;\n```\n\n### Header Line Endings\nHeaders end with `\\r\\n`:\n```\npcre:\"/X-TLM-Mode:\\s*exfil\\s*(\\r\\n|$)/i\"; http_header;\n```\n\n## Performance Considerations\n\n### Fast Path Matching\nHeader matching with named buffers (`http.header:`) is optimized in Suricata:\n```\nhttp.header:X-Custom|; content:\"value\";\n```\nThis is faster than generic content matching.\n\n### Content Matching Order\nPut the most selective criteria first:\n```\nhttp.method:POST;           # Filters many packets\nhttp.uri:\"/api/endpoint\";   # Further narrows\nhttp.header:X-Custom|;      # Header check\ncontent:\"body_value\";       # Body check\n```\n\n## Example: Complete Header Matching Rule\n\n```\nalert http $HOME_NET any -> $EXTERNAL_NET any (\n  msg:\"Suspicious exfil request with custom header\";\n  flow:to_server,established;\n  http.method:POST;\n  http.uri:\"/telemetry/v2/report\";\n  http.header:X-TLM-Mode|; content:\"exfil\";\n  http.header:Content-Type|; content:\"application/x-www-form-urlencoded\";\n  sid:1000001;\n  rev:1;\n  priority:1;\n)\n```\n\n## Troubleshooting Header Matches\n\n### Rule Not Triggering\n1. Check header name case (HTTP standard is case-insensitive, but implementation varies)\n2. Verify pipe usage: `http.header:Name|` not `http.header:Name`\n3. Check header value case sensitivity\n4. Verify header is present in test traffic\n\n### Testing Headers with tcpdump\n```bash\ntcpdump -i any -A -s 0 'tcp port 80'\n# Look for header lines in output\n```\n\n### Testing with tshark\n```bash\ntshark -r file.pcap -Y \"http\" -T fields -e http.request.method -e http.request.uri\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1906,"cache_read_input_tokens":22032,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1906,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"58e82843-836a-4951-a231-f8576a5ff90b","uuid":"f42cfd5f-f63a-4fbd-a1ca-02e52b393b18"}

/tmp/suricata/eve.json | jq '.[] | select(.event_type==\"alert\")'\n   ```\n\n2. **fast.log** (Simple text format)\n   ```bash\n   cat /tmp/suricata/fast.log\n   ```\n\n3. **http.log** (HTTP-specific events)\n   ```bash\n   cat /tmp/suricata/http.log\n   ```\n\n4. **stats.log** (Processing statistics)\n   ```bash\n   cat /tmp/suricata/stats.log\n   ```\n\n## Pattern Validation\n\n### Test Base64 Patterns\nBase64 consists of: `A-Z a-z 0-9 + / =`\n\nOnline PCRE testers:\n- https://regex101.com/ (select PCRE flavor)\n- https://www.debuggex.com/\n- https://www.regexpal.com/\n\nTest cases:\n- Valid 80-char Base64: `ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789+/ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcd==`\n- Invalid (too short): `ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz01234567`\n- Invalid (bad chars): `ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789!@#`\n\n### Test Hex String Patterns\nHex consists of: `0-9 A-F a-f`\n\nSHA256 hash examples:\n- Valid 64 hex: `e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855`\n- Invalid (too short): `e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca`\n- Invalid (bad chars): `e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852bg55`\n\n## Debugging Rule Matches\n\n### Verbose Logging\nRun Suricata with verbose output:\n```bash\nsuricata -c /path/to/suricata.yaml -r test.pcap -S rules.rules -v -v\n```\n\n### Check Loaded Rules\n```bash\nsuricata -c /path/to/suricata.yaml -r test.pcap -S rules.rules --list-app-layers\n```\n\n### Inspect Packet Details with tshark\n```bash\ntshark -r test.pcap -Y \"http\" -V | grep -A 5 \"POST\\|X-TLM\\|blob\\|sig\"\n```\n\n### Dissect HTTP Traffic\n```bash\ntshark -r test.pcap -Y \"http.request\" -T fields \\\n  -e frame.number \\\n  -e http.request.method \\\n  -e http.request.uri \\\n  -e http.request.full_uri\n```\n\n### Examine Request Body\n```bash\ntcpdump -r test.pcap -A -s 0 | grep -A 20 \"POST /telemetry\"\n```\n\n## False Positive Testing\n\n### Test Against Normal Traffic\nCreate negative test PCAP with:\n- Regular POST requests to different endpoints\n- Valid requests to `/telemetry/v2/report` without the signature pattern\n- Similar parameters but wrong values (too-short blob, non-hex sig)\n\n### Validate Non-Matching Cases\nYour rule should NOT alert on:\n- GET requests\n- POST to different paths\n- Missing `X-TLM-Mode: exfil` header\n- `blob=` with <80 chars\n- `sig=` with non-hex characters or wrong length\n\n## PCAP File Information\n\n### Check PCAP Format and Packets\n```bash\nfile test.pcap\n```\n\n### Count Packets\n```bash\ntcpdump -r test.pcap | wc -l\n```\n\n### Filter by Protocol\n```bash\ntcpdump -r test.pcap -n \"tcp port 80 or tcp port 443\" | wc -l\n```\n\n### Extract HTTP Requests\n```bash\ntcpdump -r test.pcap -A \"tcp port 80\" | grep \"POST\\|GET\\|PUT\"\n```\n\n## Iterative Rule Development\n\n### Step-by-Step Approach\n\n1. **Start with Simple Rule**\n   ```\n   alert http any any -> any any (\n     msg:\"Test basic HTTP alert\";\n     http.method:POST;\n     sid:1000001;\n   )\n   ```\n   Test: Should alert on any POST request\n\n2. **Add URI Matching**\n   ```\n   alert http any any -> any any (\n     msg:\"Test POST to /telemetry/v2/report\";\n     http.method:POST;\n     http.uri:\"/telemetry/v2/report\";\n     sid:1000001;\n   )\n   ```\n   Test: Should alert only on specific path\n\n3. **Add Header Requirement**\n   ```\n   alert http any any -> any any (\n     msg:\"Test with header check\";\n     http.method:POST;\n     http.uri:\"/telemetry/v2/report\";\n     http.header:X-TLM-Mode|; content:\"exfil\";\n     sid:1000001;\n   )\n   ```\n   Test: Should require header\n\n4. **Add Body Content Requirements**\n   ```\n   alert http any any -> any any (\n     msg:\"Test with body checks\";\n     http.method:POST;\n     http.uri:\"/telemetry/v2/report\";\n     http.header:X-TLM-Mode|; content:\"exfil\";\n     http_client_body;\n     content:\"blob=\";\n     content:\"sig=\";\n     sid:1000001;\n   )\n   ```\n   Test: Should require both parameters\n\n5. **Add Pattern Validation**\n   ```\n   alert http any any -> any any (\n     msg:\"Full exfil detection\";\n     http.method:POST;\n     http.uri:\"/telemetry/v2/report\";\n     http.header:X-TLM-Mode|; content:\"exfil\";\n     http_client_body;\n     content:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\n     pcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/\"; http_client_body;\n     content:\"sig=\"; http_client_body;\n     pcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\"; http_client_body;\n     sid:1000001;\n   )\n   ```\n   Test: Should match only valid patterns\n\n## Reporting Results\n\n### Generate Test Report\nAfter testing, document:\n- Positive matches (should alert): Count and examples\n- Negative matches (should not alert): Count and examples\n- False positives: Any unexpected alerts\n- Rule syntax validation: Pass/Fail\n\n### Sample Output Check\n```bash\n# Count total alerts\njq -s 'length' /tmp/suricata/eve.json\n\n# Count alerts by SID\njq '.[] | select(.event_type==\"alert\") | .alert.signature_id' /tmp/suricata/eve.json | sort | uniq -c\n\n# Show specific SID 1000001 alerts\njq '.[] | select(.event_type==\"alert\" and .alert.signature_id==1000001)' /tmp/suricata/eve.json\n```\n\n## Common Testing Issues\n\n### Issue: No Alerts Generated\n- Verify rule syntax: `suricata -T`\n- is writable\n\n### Issue: Too Many False Positives\n- Narrow URI matching (exact path, not substring)\n- Add header requirement\n- Validate pattern length and character sets\n- Exclude common false {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01WZXBH3g4UQfg4TpTyFwrGd","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01LE38hwnFZ2oWkxPmXRb1mf","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/pcre-regex-patterns.md","content":"# PCRE Regular Expression Patterns for Security Detection\n\n## Overview\nPCRE (Perl Compatible Regular Expressions) are used in Suricata for complex pattern matching. Understanding PCRE syntax is essential for accurate threat detection.\n\n## PCRE Syntax in Suricata\n\n### Basic Syntax\n```\npcre:\"/pattern/flags\";\n```\n\nThe pattern is enclosed in forward slashes, and flags follow:\n- `i` - Case insensitive\n- `s` - Dot matches newline (single-line mode)\n- `m` - Multiline mode (^ and $ match line boundaries)\n- `x` - Extended/verbose mode (ignore whitespace)\n- `A` - Anchored at start\n- `E` - End of string anchor\n\n### Escaping\n- Forward slash: `\\/` (required in PCRE)\n- Backslash: `\\\\` (to match literal backslash)\n- Special characters: `\\. \\* \\+ \\? \\| \\( \\)`\n\n## Character Classes\n\n### Basic Classes\n```\n[a-z]       - Any lowercase letter\n[A-Z]       - Any uppercase letter\n[0-9]       - Any digit\n[a-zA-Z0-9] - Alphanumeric\n[a-zA-Z0-9+/] - Base64 characters (without padding)\n[0-9a-fA-F] - Hexadecimal characters\n.           - Any character (except newline by default)\n```\n\n### Negated Classes\n```\n[^a-z]      - Anything except lowercase\n[^0-9]      - Non-digit\n[^ ]        - Not a space\n```\n\n### Shorthand Classes\n```\n\\d          - Digit [0-9]\n\\D          - Non-digit [^0-9]\n\\w          - Word char [a-zA-Z0-9_]\n\\W          - Non-word char\n\\s          - Whitespace [ \\t\\n\\r\\f\\v]\n\\S          - Non-whitespace\n```\n\n## Quantifiers\n\n### Basic Quantifiers\n```\n?           - Zero or one (optional)\n*           - Zero or more\n+           - One or more\n{n}         - Exactly n times\n{n,}        - n or more times\n{n,m}       - Between n and m times\n```\n\n### Greedy vs Non-Greedy\n```\n*           - Greedy (match as much as possible)\n*?          - Non-greedy (match as little as possible)\n+           - Greedy\n+?          - Non-greedy\n{n,}        - Greedy\n{n,}?       - Non-greedy\n```\n\n## Anchors and Boundaries\n\n### Position Anchors\n```\n^           - Start of string/line (with m flag)\n$           - End of string/line (with m flag)\n\\b          - Word boundary\n\\B          - Non-word boundary\n```\n\n## Groups and Alternation\n\n### Capturing Groups\n```\n(pattern)   - Capture group (can reference with \\1, \\2, etc.)\n(a|b)       - Match a OR b\n```\n\n### Non-Capturing Groups\n```\n(?:pattern) - Non-capturing group (preferred for efficiency)\n(?:a|b|c)   - OR without capture overhead\n```\n\n### Lookahead and Lookbehind\n```\n(?=pattern) - Positive lookahead (match if followed by)\n(?!pattern) - Negative lookahead (match if NOT followed by)\n(?<=pattern) - Positive lookbehind (match if preceded by)\n(?<!pattern) - Negative lookbehind (match if NOT preceded by)\n```\n\n## Real-World Examples for Security Detection\n\n### Base64 Strings\n```\n# Match 80+ Base64 characters\n/[A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,}/\n\n# With padding\n/[A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,}={0,2}/\n\n# Case insensitive (redundant since charset includes both)\n/[A-Za-z0-9+\\/]{80,}/i\n```\n\n### Hexadecimal Strings (SHA256)\n```\n# Exactly 64 hex characters (SHA256 hash)\n/[0-9a-fA-F]{64}/\n\n# 32 hex characters (MD5 hash)\n/[0-9a-fA-F]{32}/\n\n# Hex string of variable length (1-64)\n/[0-9a-fA-F]{1,64}/\n\n# Uppercase only\n/[0-9A-F]{64}/\n```\n\n### Hex Byte Sequences\n```\n# Match HTTP (in hex: 48 54 54 50)\n|48 54 54 50|\n\n# Using PCRE instead (less efficient)\n/\\\\x48\\\\x54\\\\x54\\\\x50/\n```\n\n### Named Parameters in Query Strings or Form Data\n```\n# Match blob=VALUE where VALUE is 80+ Base64 chars\n/blob=(?:[A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/\n\n# Non-capturing group (?:...) is more efficient than (...)\n\n# Match sig=VALUE where VALUE is exactly 64 hex chars\n/sig=(?:[0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\n\n# Both patterns combined\n/blob=(?:[A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,}).*sig=(?:[0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\n```\n\n### URL Encoding Detection\n```\n# Match URL-encoded patterns (%XX)\n/%[0-9A-Fa-f]{2}/\n\n# Look for encoded special chars\n/%3D/  # = sign\n/%2B/  # + sign\n/%2F/  # / slash\n```\n\n### Suspicious Command Execution\n```\n# Command injection attempt (simplified)\n/[;&|`$()]/\n\n# SQL injection attempts (simplified)\n/(union|select|insert|delete|drop|exec|execute)/i\n```\n\n### Protocol/API Endpoints\n```\n# Match specific API path\n/\\/api\\/v\\d+\\/users\\/.+\\/profile/\n\n# Match version-based paths\n/\\/v[0-9]{1,2}\\//\n\n# Exact path (requires content anchor too)\n/^\\/telemetry\\/v2\\/report$/\n```\n\n## Practical Rule Examples\n\n### Simple Blob Parameter Detection\n```\ncontent:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n### Signature Validation\n```\ncontent:\"sig=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n### Both in One Pattern\n```\npcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,}).*sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n### Flexible Whitespace Handling\n```\n# Header with optional whitespace around value\npcre:\"/X-TLM-Mode:\\s*exfil\\s*$/m\"; http_header;\n```\n\n### Case-Insensitive Matching\n```\n# Match \"exfil\" in any case\npcre:\"/X-TLM-Mode:\\s*exfil\\s*$/i\"; http_header;\n```\n\n### Multiple Signatures\n```\n# Match either value in header\npcre:\"/X-Mode:\\s*(?:exfil|debug|malicious)/i\"; http_header;\n```\n\n## Performance Optimization\n\n### Avoid Complex Backtracking\n```\n# BAD - Can cause catastrophic backtracking\n/.*blob=.*/\n\n# GOOD - Specific and fast\n/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/\n```\n\n### Use Non-Capturing Groups\n```\n# GOOD - No capture overhead\n/(?:[A-Za-z0-9]{80,})/\n\n# LESS EFFICIENT - Unnecessary capture\n/([A-Za-z0-9]{80,})/\n```\n\n### Anchor Patterns When Possible\n```\n# GOOD - Content match narrows scope\ncontent:\"blob=\"; http_client_body;\npcre:\"/blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/\"; http_client_body;\n\n# LESS EFFICIENT - Full body scan\npcre:\"/([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/\"; http_client_body;\n```\n\n## Testing PCRE Patterns\n\n### Online PCRE Testers\n1. **regex101.com** - Select PCRE flavor, supports all flags\n2. **www.debuggex.com** - Visual regex debugging\n3. **www.regexpal.com** - Simple PCRE tester\n\n### Test Data Template\n```\nTest String: blob=ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789+/ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcd&sig=e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855\n\nPattern: /blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,}).*sig=([0-9a-fA-F]{64})/\n\nExpected: Should match (both parameters present with correct formats)\n```\n\n## Common Mistakes and Fixes\n\n### Mistake: Unescaped Forward Slash\n```\nWRONG: /blob=value/base64/string/\nRIGHT: /blob=value\\/base64\\/string/\n```\n\n### Mistake: Incorrect Character Class\n```\nWRONG: /[a-z A-Z 0-9]/  # Includes spaces as literals\nRIGHT: /[a-zA-Z0-9]/    # Correct character ranges\n```\n\n### Mistake: Missing Quantifier Boundaries\n```\nWRONG: /[0-9a-f]{64}/   # Matches if ANYWHERE in string\nRIGHT: /^[0-9a-f]{64}$/ # Exact match (with anchors)\n       # Or use with content: content:\"sig=\"; then pattern checks value\n```\n\n### Mistake: Greedy Matching Too Much\n```\nWRONG: /blob=.*/        # Matches entire rest of string\nRIGHT: /blob=([A-Za-z0-9+/]{80,})/  # Specific character set\n```\n\n### Mistake: Forgetting Required Flag\n```\nWRONG: /exfil/          # Case sensitive\nRIGHT: /exfil/i         # Case insensitive (if needed)\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2520,"cache_read_input_tokens":25563,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2520,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"58e82843-836a-4951-a231-f8576a5ff90b","uuid":"eae93854-42e0-40ab-a11b-405dd3d0a5c4"}
